Official live-style sizing + compound growth

Live buy size (every getTarget system, including RS / BRT / YH / WPBR — not only 6-sys): risk = min(1% beginning-of-month equity, $50k), shares ≤ 1% ADV20, notional ≤ 17.5% of current equity. The $250k vs S&P 500 (SPY) wallet below is the official six-sleeve compound book (StockBee, Relative Strength Index, Volume Zone, Magic Touch, Rocket Launcher, Weekly Range / Swing; Indicators out). DailyRun wire / official mix change — not gold. Old five-sleeve pin is leftover only. Uses official shares × house Closed fill prices — not dummy $10k / $47.5k notionals. See compound growth (live-style) · wallet monthly · investment report Suggested shares / avg-cost lots. Engine Closed rows further down still use house dummy notionals for reconcile.

WRL · -$662 · 1 closed

SymbolOpenedClosedExitDaysPnL %PnL $
NE2026-09-302026-10-01STOP_LOSS1-1.99%-$662

Open positions (unrealized)

Current backtest open book from latest runs — what each system would show if held through the latest price update.

Recommended shares use the same live buy-size rules as the investment report (freeze risk_1pct_50k_adv_17name_20260917), sized off $245,000 starting balance. Risk dollars are the smaller of 1% of that $245,000 or $50,000. Share count also stays within 1% of 20-day average daily volume (ADV20), and the position cannot use more than 17.5% of the $245,000 (the name cap). Relative Strength Index (RSI) uses the frozen average-loss slot instead of the distance from entry to the stop. The share count is a range until the order fills (next-open band, the day’s high–low, or last close ± Average True Range (ATR)). Size lid names the rule that set the count: risk_1pct (1% of the starting balance), risk_50k ($50,000 risk ceiling), adv (ADV20), or name_cap (17.5% of $245,000). A range: prefix means the two ends of the fill band hit different lids. If the same ticker is open in more than one system, systems further down this page only get the name-cap room left after earlier systems. Dollar P&L in the other columns is still the house dummy size.

Stop loss and Target are the prices already on each system’s latest open book (Stop price and Target price, or Weekly Range (WRL) Stop loss and Target). Rocket Launcher (RL) records a static target: 20% above the buy until the high reaches that price (sell 80% of the shares), then 40% above the buy for the leftover shares. It does not use a moving 50-day average target. The stop stays the original signal-bar stop until that 20% high prints, then moves to the buy price if that is tighter. An older open book that still has a zero target is filled the same way. Hover a price to see where it came from. Click column headers to sort.
